I mean, I don't feel sad or depressed or anything like that. I'm just frustrated with myself over how I handled that call. It's just not who I am. But no, I wouldn't say I've been feeling down.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself acting on impulse more often lately?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. Honestly, aside from that one moment on the call, I've been conducting business as usual. I'm not making rash decisions or doing things without thinking them through. It was really just that one outburst.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your relationships with friends or family members lately?\n\nPatient: No, I... I haven't noticed anything like that. Things at home are fine. My family hasn't mentioned me acting any differently, and I'm not having any issues with friends or anything. Like I said, my life is pretty much exactly the same as always, except for that one incident at work.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any physical complaints lately?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. Physically, I feel completely fine. I haven't had any aches or pains or... well, any issues, really. Like I keep saying, my health is good and everything is normal. It was really just that one situation at work.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel any unusual emotions in the days or hours leading up to that board call?\n\nPatient: No, I didn't. I felt completely normal before the call. I was just reviewing my notes and getting ready for the meeting like I always do. There wasn't anything out of the ordinary going on with me emotionally or otherwise.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an outburst like that before?\n\nPatient: No, never. I... I don't do that. I've been in charge for a lot of years, and I always keep a level head. I've never lost control like that in a meeting before. It was a first.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself being indirectly resistant or resentful towards others in the past?\n\nPatient: No, I... I can't say that I have. If I have a problem with someone, I just address it with them right then and there. I don't really do the whole... holding onto resentment or dragging my feet on things. It's just not how I operate. I've always preferred to just deal with problems head-on.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself doing something on purpose to annoy or frustrate someone, without directly confronting them?\n\nPatient: No, I... I don't play those kinds of games. Like I was saying before, I just don't operate that way. If I'm frustrated with someone, they're going to hear about it directly from me. I really don't have the time or the patience to sneak around doing little things just to annoy people.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself being passive-aggressive towards someone?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, passive-aggressive... that's just another way of not dealing with things directly, right? So, no. Like I said, I'm pretty straightforward. If there's an issue, I just say it. I don't really see the point in beating around the bush.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any times in your personal relationships where you might have expressed negative feelings indirectly?\n\nPatient: Honestly, doc, I feel like we're just covering the same ground here. No, not in my personal relationships either. Whether it's with my family or my friends, if I'm upset about something, I tell them. I don't drop hints or... or give people the silent treatment. I just say what's on my mind and get it over with.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were being forced to do something you didn't want to do, and then did it in a way that was intentionally less than perfect?\n\nPatient: No, I can't say that I have. Look, in my line of work, if you don't want to do something, you either delegate it or you just say no. And if I do commit to something, I do it right. Doing a half-baked job just to prove a point... it's just bad business. It reflects poorly on me. So, no, I don't do that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were outside of your body watching yourself during a stressful situation?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, actually... that has happened recently. Like I said, my life is pretty much exactly the same as always, except for that one incident at work.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any physical complaints lately?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. Physically, I feel completely fine. I haven't had any aches or pains or... well, any issues, really. Like I keep saying, my health is good and everything is normal. It was really just that one situation at work.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel any unusual emotions in the days or hours leading up to that board call?\n\nPatient: No, I didn't. I felt completely normal before the call. I was just reviewing my notes and getting ready for the meeting like I always do. There wasn't anything out of the ordinary going on with me emotionally or otherwise.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an outburst like that before?\n\nPatient: No, never. I... I don't do that. I've been in charge for a lot of years, and I always keep a level head. I've never lost control like that in a meeting before. It was a first.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself being indirectly resistant or resentful towards others in the past?\n\nPatient: No, I... I can't say that I have. If I have a problem with someone, I just address it with them right then and there. I don't really do the whole... holding onto resentment or dragging my feet on things. It's just not how I operate. I've always preferred to just deal with problems head-on.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself doing something on purpose to annoy or frustrate someone, without directly confronting them?\n\nPatient: No, I... I don't play those kinds of games. Like I was saying before, I just don't operate that way. If I'm frustrated with someone, they're going to hear about it directly from me. I really don't have the time or the patience to sneak around doing little things just to annoy people.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself being passive-aggressive towards someone?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, passive-aggressive... that's just another way of not dealing with things directly, right? So, no. Like I said, I'm pretty straightforward. If there's an issue, I just say it. I don't really see the point in beating around the bush.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any times in your personal relationships where you might have expressed negative feelings indirectly?\n\nPatient: Honestly, doc, I feel like we're just covering the same ground here. No, not in my personal relationships either. Whether it's with my family or my friends, if I'm upset about something, I tell them. I don't drop hints or... or give people the silent treatment. I just say what's on my mind and get it over with.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were being forced to do something you didn't want to do, and then did it in a way that was intentionally less than perfect?\n\nPatient: No, I can't say that I have. Look, in my line of work, if you don't want to do something, you either delegate it or you just say no. And if I do commit to something, I do it right. Doing a half-baked job just to prove a point... it's just bad business. It reflects poorly on me. So, no, I don't do that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were outside of your body watching yourself during a stressful situation?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, actually... that has happened recently. It's usually when I'm taking a call from home, especially if it's something stressful. The room just starts to look... I don't know, distant? And my own voice sounds really strange to me. It's almost like I'm sitting there watching myself talk instead of actually being the one having the conversation. It's a weird feeling.\n\nDoctor: How often have you had that feeling of being outside of your body watching yourself?\n\nPatient: I couldn't give you an exact number, to be honest. I haven't really kept a tally or anything. It's just... it's happened a handful of times lately when I'm on those stressful calls. But as for exactly how often it happens, I'm really not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like things around you weren't real?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really felt that way. The room looking distant or my voice sounding weird, sure, but I never felt like the things around me were fake or not real. It's just my own body that feels off during those moments. Everything else is just... normal, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were outside of your body watching yourself during a non-stressful situation?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's only ever happened when I'm dealing with those high-pressure calls for work. If I'm just sitting around the house or doing something normal, I feel fine. It's just the stress that seems to trigger it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were outside of your body watching yourself when you were not on a work call?\n\nPatient: No, like I said, it's really just those stressful work calls. But no, I wouldn't say I've been feeling down.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself acting on impulse more often lately?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. Honestly, aside from that one moment on the call, I've been conducting business as usual. I'm not making rash decisions or doing things without thinking them through. It was really just that one outburst.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your relationships with friends or family members lately?\n\nPatient: No, I... I haven't noticed anything like that. Things at home are fine. My family hasn't mentioned me acting any differently, and I'm not having any issues with friends or anything. Physically, I feel completely fine. I haven't had any aches or pains or... well, any issues, really. Like I keep saying, my health is good and everything is normal. It was really just that one situation at work.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel any unusual emotions in the days or hours leading up to that board call?\n\nPatient: No, I didn't. I felt completely normal before the call. I was just reviewing my notes and getting ready for the meeting like I always do. There wasn't anything out of the ordinary going on with me emotionally or otherwise.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an outburst like that before?\n\nPatient: No, never. I... I don't do that. I've been in charge for a lot of years, and I always keep a level head. I've never lost control like that in a meeting before. It was a first.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself being indirectly resistant or resentful towards others in the past?\n\nPatient: No, I... I can't say that I have. If I have a problem with someone, I just address it with them right then and there. I don't really do the whole... holding onto resentment or dragging my feet on things. It's just not how I operate. I've always preferred to just deal with problems head-on.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself doing something on purpose to annoy or frustrate someone, without directly confronting them?\n\nPatient: No, I... I don't play those kinds of games. Like I was saying before, I just don't operate that way. If I'm frustrated with someone, they're going to hear about it directly from me. I really don't have the time or the patience to sneak around doing little things just to annoy people.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself being passive-aggressive towards someone?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, passive-aggressive... that's just another way of not dealing with things directly, right? So, no. Like I said, I'm pretty straightforward. If there's an issue, I just say it. I don't really see the point in beating around the bush.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any times in your personal relationships where you might have expressed negative feelings indirectly?\n\nPatient: Honestly, doc, I feel like we're just covering the same ground here. No, not in my personal relationships either. Whether it's with my family or my friends, if I'm upset about something, I tell them. I don't drop hints or... or give people the silent treatment. I just say what's on my mind and get it over with.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were being forced to do something you didn't want to do, and then did it in a way that was intentionally less than perfect?\n\nPatient: No, I can't say that I have. Look, in my line of work, if you don't want to do something, you either delegate it or you just say no. And if I do commit to something, I do it right. Doing a half-baked job just to prove a point... it's just bad business. It reflects poorly on me. So, no, I don't do that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were outside of your body watching yourself during a stressful situation?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, actually... that has happened recently. It's usually when I'm taking a call from home, especially if it's something stressful. The room just starts to look... I don't know, distant? And my own voice sounds really strange to me. It's almost like I'm sitting there watching myself talk instead of actually being the one having the conversation. It's a weird feeling.\n\nDoctor: How often have you had that feeling of being outside of your body watching yourself?\n\nPatient: I couldn't give you an exact number, to be honest. I haven't really kept a tally or anything. It's just... it's happened a handful of times lately when I'm on those stressful calls. But as for exactly how often it happens, I'm really not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like things around you weren't real?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really felt that way. Everything else is just... normal, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were outside of your body watching yourself during a non-stressful situation?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's only ever happened when I'm dealing with those high-pressure calls for work. If I'm just sitting around the house or doing something normal, I feel fine. It's just the stress that seems to trigger it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were outside of your body watching yourself when you were not on a work call?\n\nPatient: No, like I said, it's really just those stressful work calls. If I'm not on one of those, I haven't had that feeling at all.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like you were outside of your body watching yourself during the board call when you lost your temper?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I didn't have that detached, watching-myself feeling then.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like you were in a daze or disconnected from yourself when you snapped at them during the board call?\n\nPatient: No, not when I snapped at them.
